LINK IDS — how gloss-ref links work, how every ID on this site is built,
what's been learned doing full linking passes, and a complete ID reference
=============================================================================

WHAT THIS FILE IS FOR
----------------------
The *-data.js files (spells, classes, feats, lore, rules-glossary,
conditions, rules, items, magic-items, monsters) are too large to sit in the project's context window,
so their content isn't visible by default. This file documents the ID
patterns those files actually use, the judgment calls learned from doing
full "linking passes" over site content, and — since a complete linking
pass has now been done and every ID extracted — a full reference list of
every ID on the site (see COMPLETE ID REFERENCE near the end). Together
these mean links can usually be added correctly WITHOUT needing to
re-upload and re-read every data file first.

If a target ID can be predicted with confidence from the patterns below,
or found directly in the COMPLETE ID REFERENCE, it can be used directly.
If there's any doubt (a compound/unusual name, a feature that might not
have its own anchor, uncertainty about which edition to link, or the ID
reference looks like it might be stale — e.g. after a data file has been
edited independently since this file was last regenerated), the actual
file should be uploaded and searched before guessing — a wrong data-ref
is a silently broken link.

KEEPING THIS FILE CURRENT: the COMPLETE ID REFERENCE section is a
snapshot. If a *-data.js file is edited (entries added/removed/renamed)
after this file's last update, the reference for that file is stale and
should be regenerated (see "REGENERATING THE ID REFERENCE" below) rather
than trusted blindly. When in doubt, re-extract rather than guess.


THE LINK MECHANIC ITSELF
--------------------------
Every internal link on the site looks like this:

    <a href="#" class="gloss-ref" data-ref="TARGET-ID">Visible Text</a>

- href="#" is always literal — never a real URL.
- class="gloss-ref" is required; it's what the site's click handler
  looks for to intercept the click and open the target instead of
  following the link normally.
- data-ref="TARGET-ID" must exactly match the id="..." of the target
  <details> element somewhere on the page (case-sensitive, exact string).
- An optional second class, "skill-italic", renders the link text in
  italics (used for skill names and creature type names specifically).
- The visible link text does NOT have to match the target's title
  word-for-word — e.g. a link reading "Thief: Second-Story Work" can
  point at an entry titled just "Second Story Work"; "Firebolt" (as
  written in flavor text) can point at the spell actually titled
  "Fire Bolt"; "Dmg. Threshold" (an abbreviated table header) can point
  at "Damage Threshold".


HOW IDS ARE BUILT — GENERAL RULES
------------------------------------
Every ID is a lowercase, hyphen-separated ("kebab-case") slug:
- Spaces become hyphens.
- Apostrophes become a literal "s" (not removed, not a hyphen):
    "Monk's Focus"      -> monk-s-focus
    "Thief's Reflexes"  -> thief-s-reflexes
- Punctuation like colons, slashes, and parentheses is generally
  dropped or replaced with hyphens depending on the file — check a
  neighboring real ID in that file if unsure, or check the COMPLETE ID
  REFERENCE below.


ID PATTERNS BY FILE
-----------------------

rules-glossary-data.js
  Pattern:  glossary-{term}
  Examples: glossary-strength, glossary-armor-class, glossary-d20-test,
            glossary-ability-check, glossary-saving-throw
  166 entries total. See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below for the full list.

conditions-data.js
  Pattern:  condition-{name}
  Examples: condition-blinded, condition-prone, condition-exhaustion
  15 entries (all official 2024/2014 conditions).

lore-data.js
  Group/container entries (Planes, Creature Types, Spell Schools, etc.):
    lore-{group-name}, e.g. lore-planes, lore-creature-types,
    lore-spell-schools, lore-outer-planes, lore-upper-planes
  Individual planes:        plane-{name}, e.g. plane-material,
                             plane-feywild, plane-shadowfell, plane-abyss
  Individual creature types: creature-type-{name}, e.g.
                             creature-type-humanoid, creature-type-undead
  Individual spell schools:  spell-school-{name}, e.g.
                             spell-school-evocation, spell-school-necromancy
  60 entries total (including group/container ids).

spells-data.js
  Pattern:  spell-{kebab-case-spell-name}
  Examples: spell-fireball, spell-jump,
            spell-abi-dalzim-s-horrid-wilting
  IMPORTANT: spells use ONE id per spell regardless of edition. When a
  spell differs between 2024 and 2014, both versions live inside the
  SAME entry's html, with the 2014 text under an <h5>Legacy Version</h5>
  sub-heading — there is no separate "-2014-" id to link to. Do not
  invent one.
  "Known by:" lines inside a spell link to the whole class via the
  class-only id pattern below (e.g. data-ref="class-2024-wizard").
  663 individual spells total (plus 24 A-Z letter-group container ids
  used by the site's own UI grouping). See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below
  for the full list (grouped alphabetically by first letter, matching
  the site's own A-Z grouping of the Spells dropdown).

classes-data.js
  Base class-only entry (used by "Known by:" links etc.):
    class-{edition}-{class}
    e.g. class-2024-wizard, class-2024-sorcerer, class-2024-rogue
  Base class feature:
    class-{edition}-{class}-level-{N}-{feature-slug}
    e.g. class-2024-barbarian-level-1-rage
  Subclass overview entry:
    class-{edition}-{class}-{subclass-slug}
    e.g. class-2024-barbarian-path-of-the-wild-heart
  Subclass feature:
    class-{edition}-{class}-{subclass-slug}-level-{N}-{feature-slug}
    e.g. class-2014-fighter-champion-level-7-remarkable-athlete
  edition is always "2024" or "2014" — never omitted, never anything else.
  STRUCTURAL NOTE (confirmed by direct extraction): base class features
  (Level 1: Rage, Level 2: Action Surge, etc.) ARE real JSON "children"
  entries of the base class entry — NOT literal nested HTML the way
  subclass content is. The "pick a subclass" level entry (e.g.
  "class-2024-fighter-level-3-fighter-subclass") is itself a JSON child,
  but ITS html field contains the subclasses and their own features as
  literal nested <details id="..."><summary>...</summary> HTML blocks —
  so subclass overviews and subclass-level features must be found by
  searching for id=\"...\" text patterns inside that entry's html, not
  by walking a further "children" array.
  A feature that's really just one bullet/paragraph inside a bigger
  feature (e.g. "Step of the Wind" is one of three abilities listed
  under the Monk's "Focus"/"Ki" feature) has NO id of its own — link to
  the parent feature entry instead.
  EDITION CHOICE ISN'T ALWAYS "2024 BY DEFAULT": if a same-named feature
  exists in both editions but the specific mechanic being referenced only
  exists in one of them (2024 sometimes drops or rewrites what a 2014
  feature did, or vice versa), link to whichever edition's text actually
  contains that mechanic — check both before assuming 2024.
  CONFIRMED EXAMPLE: the Fighter's "Two Extra Attacks" feature (its
  level-11 extra-attack upgrade) only has its own separately-named anchor
  in the 2024 data (class-2024-fighter-level-11-two-extra-attacks) — the
  2014 data never split that upgrade out with its own id/title, so even
  in an otherwise-2014-framed passage, that specific named feature must
  link to the 2024 id, because that's the only edition where an anchor
  with that exact name exists at all.
  1,876 class/subclass/feature entries total. See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE
  below for the full list, grouped by edition then by class in the
  order features appear in-game (base class, then its level-by-level
  features, then each subclass and its features).

feats-data.js
  Pattern:  feat-{kebab-case-feat-name}
  Example:  feat-athlete
  Most feats are a SINGLE merged entry covering both editions' text (no
  "-2024-"/"-2014-" split) — confirm this per-feat rather than assuming,
  since a few other content types on the site do split by edition.
  249 individual feats total (plus 7 category container ids, pattern
  feat-cat-{category}), in 7 categories (Origin, Fighting Style, General,
  Dragonmark, Planar Pact, Dark Gift, Epic Boon). See COMPLETE ID
  REFERENCE below for the full list.

rules-data.js (this project's own file, not sourced from a rulebook)
  Flat entries use plain dropX-style ids (dropCombat, dropCover, etc.);
  grouped topics use rules-{name} containers with rules-{name}-{sub}
  children (e.g. rules-magic-item-tables containing
  rules-magic-item-table-a through rules-magic-item-table-i;
  rules-businesses containing rules-business-downtime-profits and
  rules-business-alternative-profits). The downtimeActivities array is
  a SEPARATE top-level array from entries (both render into the Rules
  dropdown, but downtimeActivities renders nested one level down, inside
  the hand-written "Downtime Activities" dropdown in index.html).
  Some Rules-dropdown content is hand-written directly in index.html
  instead of living in rules-data.js, because it needs live inputs/
  buttons that plain data can't drive: dropCarryingEncumbrance,
  dropJumping, dropNpcReactions, and six hand-written downtime entries
  nested in index.html's own downtimeActivitiesContainer alongside the
  data-driven ones (rules-downtime-buy-magic-item, rules-downtime-
  carousing, rules-downtime-crime, rules-downtime-gambling, rules-
  downtime-pit-fighting, rules-downtime-research).
  61 rules-data.js ids + these hand-written index.html ids. See
  COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below for the full list.

items-data.js  (Equipment — all non-magical gear; renders inside the "Items" dropdown)
  Root:            items-equipment  (container, empty html)
  Categories:      equipment-{category}   e.g. equipment-adventuring-gear,
                   equipment-weapons, equipment-armor, equipment-tools,
                   equipment-mounts, equipment-packs
                   (Airborne & Waterborne Vehicles = equipment-vehicles-waterborne;
                   Food, Drink & Lodging = equipment-food-drink-lodging; Barding and
                   Trade Goods are single table entries with no sub-entries)
  Sub-categories:  equipment-tools-artisans / -gaming-sets / -musical-instruments /
                   -other, equipment-weapons-simple-melee / -simple-ranged /
                   -martial-melee / -martial-ranged, equipment-weapon-properties,
                   equipment-weapon-mastery-properties, equipment-arcane-foci,
                   equipment-druidic-foci, equipment-holy-symbols
  Items:           equipment-{kebab-title}   e.g. equipment-longsword,
                   equipment-alchemist-s-fire, equipment-thieves-tools
                   A comma in the title is dropped:  "Bottle, Glass" ->
                   equipment-bottle-glass.  Parentheses are dropped:  "Rod (Focus)"
                   -> equipment-rod-focus.
  Armor:           the body armors carry an "-armor" suffix (equipment-padded-armor,
                   equipment-chain-mail-armor, equipment-plate-armor); the Shield is
                   equipment-shield; the Strength-requirement note is
                   equipment-armor-strength-requirement.
  Weapon properties and masteries (the 2024 weapon rules text that weapon entries
                   reference): equipment-property-{name}  (ammunition, finesse,
                   heavy, light, loading, range, reach, thrown, two-handed,
                   versatile) and equipment-mastery-{name}  (cleave, graze, nick,
                   push, sap, slow, topple, vex).
  Same entry for both editions: when an item differs in 2014 the old text sits
  inside the SAME entry under <h5>Legacy Version (2014)</h5>. There is never a
  separate 2014 id.
  226 entries inside 250 nodes (24 containers). See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below.

magic-items-data.js  (Magic Items — also holds ALL poisons, which are filed here;
                   renders inside the "Items" dropdown as a sibling of Equipment)
  Root:            items-magic-items  (container, empty html; the file is ONE root
                   entry, not the usual {intro, entries} wrapper)
  Categories:      magic-items-{category}   (12): magic-items-alchemical-items,
                   -ammunition, -armor, -poisons, -potions, -rings, -rods,
                   -scrolls, -staves, -wands, -weapons, -wondrous-items
  Rarity buckets:  magic-items-{category}-{rarity}   e.g.
                   magic-items-weapons-uncommon, magic-items-potions-very-rare,
                   magic-items-wands-artifact   (rarities: common, uncommon, rare,
                   very-rare, legendary, artifact — only the ones a category
                   uses exist). Scrolls has NO buckets: its three entries sit
                   directly under magic-items-scrolls.
  Items:           magic-item-{kebab-title}   e.g. magic-item-bag-of-holding,
                   magic-item-flame-tongue, magic-item-heward-s-handy-haversack
                   (an apostrophe becomes a hyphen, so "Heward's" = heward-s and
                   "Armourer's" = armourer-s)
  Tiered items:    one entry per rarity tier, id ends with the bonus number:
                   magic-item-weapon-1/-2/-3, magic-item-armor-1/-2/-3,
                   magic-item-shield-1/-2/-3, magic-item-ammunition-1/-2/-3,
                   magic-item-rod-of-the-pact-keeper-1/-2/-3,
                   magic-item-wand-of-the-war-mage-1/-2/-3,
                   magic-item-wraps-of-unarmed-power-1/-2/-3
  Enspelled items: magic-item-enspelled-{weapon|armor|staff}-{uncommon|rare|
                   very-rare|legendary}  (one per rarity tier, level table inside)
  Variant items:   Ioun Stones, Instruments of the Bards, Figurines of Wondrous
                   Power, Quaal's Feather Tokens, Crystal Balls, Belts of Giant
                   Strength, Potions of Giant Strength, Potions of Healing... each
                   variant has its own entry (see ITEM LINKING NOTES below)
  Eberron items:   33 entries from Eberron: Forge of the Artificer (9: Boots of
                   the Winding Path, Dazzling Weapon, Helm of Awareness, Manifold
                   Tool, Mind Sharpener, Repeating Shot, Repulsion Shield,
                   Returning Weapon, Spell-Refueling Ring) and Eberron: Rising
                   from the Last War (24 items, 25 entries; Prosthetic Limb was
                   already present). Ids follow the normal magic-item-{title}
                   pattern, e.g. magic-item-docent, magic-item-keycharm,
                   magic-item-dyrrn-s-tentacle-whip.
  Poisons that keep an equipment- id: the 14 official poisons are inside this
                   file but their ids start with "equipment-" (NOT "magic-item-"):
                   equipment-assassin-s-blood, equipment-malice,
                   equipment-pale-tincture, equipment-serpent-venom,
                   equipment-truth-serum, equipment-burnt-othur-fumes,
                   equipment-essence-of-ether, equipment-lolth-s-sting,
                   equipment-oil-of-taggit, equipment-torpor,
                   equipment-carrion-crawler-mucus, equipment-midnight-tears,
                   equipment-purple-worm-poison, equipment-wyvern-poison.
                   Never rename them. Use them whenever one of those poisons is
                   named.
  Same entry for both editions: 2014 text sits inside the same entry under
  <h5>Legacy Version (2014)</h5>. There is never a separate 2014 id.
  559 entries inside 625 nodes (66 containers). See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below.

monsters-data.js  (Monsters — stat blocks in the 2024 layout; renders in the "Monsters" dropdown)
  Containers:      monsters-letter-{a-z}  (A-Z grouping, empty html, same idea as spells)
  Monsters:        monster-{kebab-title}  e.g. monster-aboleth, monster-adult-bronze-dragon
                   Apostrophes follow the usual rule (apostrophe -> hyphen, repeated
                   hyphens collapse); accents are dropped.
  Sources: the creatures in the Improved Initiative export, its built-in Basic Rules set, and the ten Open5e
                   creature books (5e 2014 Rules, 5e 2024 Rules, Tome of Beasts 1/2/3 and the 2023 edition of 1,
                   Creature Codex, Black Flag SRD, Monstrous Menagerie, Tal'dorei Campaign Setting).
  Same-name stat blocks: identical copies were merged into one. When two versions of
                   the same name really differ, the first keeps the plain title and the
                   other gets its source (or a number) in the title, and the id follows
                   the title: monster-aboleth, monster-aboleth-basic-rules, monster-archer-2.
  4,729 monsters + 26 letter containers = 4,755 entries. See COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below.

Top-level Reference-pane dropdown wrappers (for "see the X dropdown
above" style links, already used inside rules-glossary-data.js):
  dropRules, dropConditions, dropNpcReactions, dropRulesGlossary,
  dropLore, dropSpells, dropClasses, dropFeats, dropItems, dropMonsters
  These whole-dropdown ids are also valid link targets in their own
  right for cross-referencing an entire section from elsewhere — e.g.
  Travel and Pace's "Mounts" callout links to dropMountedCombat, and
  Training's "learning a language" links to dropLanguages, as a
  navigational shortcut rather than linking a specific glossary term.


LINKING METHODOLOGY — CONTEXT-EVALUATION HEURISTICS
--------------------------------------------------------
Learned from doing a full context-aware linking pass over the entire
Rules dropdown (rules-data.js + the hand-written Rules blocks in
index.html). These supplement (don't replace) the core rule: every
potential link must be evaluated for whether the word is being used in
its specific rules-mechanical sense here, not just because the text
matches a target's title.

1. PROPER NOUNS LINK EVERY TIME THEY'RE NAMED.
   Named spells, feats, specific class features, specific subclasses,
   and specific planes are precise, low-ambiguity references. Link them
   EVERY time they're mentioned by name in a mechanical/informative
   context, even if that means the same spell or feat gets linked
   several times across one entry (e.g. "Mounted Combatant" was linked
   four separate times across the Mounted Combat entry, since each
   mention was a fresh, informative reference to that specific feat).

2. COMMON RULES WORDS GET RESTRAINED, NOT REPEATED, LINKING.
   Ultra-common mechanical nouns (damage, creature, object, action,
   Speed, Prone, Reaction, Hit Points, DC) appear constantly throughout
   nearly every entry. Linking every single occurrence produces a wall
   of blue with no added value. The site's own existing content (e.g.
   glossary-malnutrition, glossary-damage-threshold) confirms the
   convention: link the first meaningful occurrence, and allow a second
   link later in the same entry if it's a genuinely fresh, distinct
   statement of a different fact — but skip a third+ repeat in tight
   proximity, and always skip a repeat that's purely anaphoric ("this
   Exhaustion", "the same saving throw", "the object" referring straight
   back to something just named). A useful rule of thumb on a long,
   multi-section entry: allow one such link per distinct <h5>-headed
   subsection rather than per whole entry, since each subsection reads
   almost like its own mini-block.

3. TABLE HEADERS CAN BE RE-LINKED ACROSS SEPARATE TABLES, BUT NOT
   WITHIN ONE HEAVILY REPEATED TABLE.
   When the same header (e.g. "Survival DC", "Bonus Action", "AC") is
   the header of several genuinely separate tables in the same entry
   (2-4 of them), link it fresh in each table — each is a distinct
   visual block a reader might jump straight to. But when a table
   structure repeats many times over (e.g. "Tool Proficiency" as a
   column header across 15+ equipment sub-tables in Craft an Item),
   link only the first real occurrence and leave the rest bare.

4. BARE "save" IS NOT LINKED; "saving throw" IS.
   The site's own precedent (glossary-malnutrition: "Constitution
   save") leaves the shorthand "save" unlinked even next to an ability
   score, but full "saving throw"/"saving throws" phrasing links to
   glossary-saving-throw. Match this distinction rather than linking
   every appearance of the word "save".

5. COMPOUND HEADERS/PHRASES LINK ONLY THEIR MATCHING PART.
   "Creature Size" links just "Size"; "Dmg. Threshold" links to Damage
   Threshold even though the visible text is abbreviated; "Escape a
   Grapple" links just "Grapple" to glossary-grappling. Don't force an
   exact-text match — link the specific word(s) that carry the
   rules-mechanical meaning, leave the rest of the phrase as plain text.

6. VERB FORMS OF A CONDITION CAN LINK TO THE CONDITION.
   Descriptive verbs that clearly describe a condition being imposed
   ("could blind everyone nearby", "jaws restrain a character") were
   linked to the matching condition (condition-blinded, condition-
   restrained) even though the condition's own title is the adjective
   form ("Blinded", "Restrained"). This is a legitimate, non-literal
   text match because the mechanical meaning is exact.

7. EDITION SELECTION FOR classes-data.js NEEDS A CONTENT CHECK, NOT A
   BLANKET RULE.
   When a whole entry is clearly framed around one edition's specific
   mechanics (e.g. rules-data.js's Multiclassing entry describes the
   13-ability-score multiclass prerequisite, which only exists in 2014
   — 2024 dropped ability-score prerequisites for multiclassing
   entirely), default every class-name link in that entry to that
   edition for consistency — EXCEPT where a specific named feature only
   has its own anchor in the other edition (see the Fighter "Two Extra
   Attacks" example above), in which case follow the anchor, not the
   surrounding framing.

8. KNOWN GAPS — TERMS WITH NO VALID TARGET ON THIS SITE.
   Don't force a link for these; there is nowhere for them to go:
     - DM / Dungeon Master (mentioned only inline inside
       glossary-abbreviations' own text, not its own anchor)
     - Rarity, Price (no dedicated glossary entries)
     - Magic items that have no entry in magic-items-data.js (anything
       outside the DMG / Xanathar's / 2024 DMG baseline, the Piccolo917
       additions, and the Eberron additions (Eberron: Rising from the Last
       War and Eberron: Forge of the Artificer): Tasha's, Fizban's, Glory of the Giants, Book of Many
       Things and adventure artifacts, plus items named only as stat-block
       gear). Items that DO have an entry link to it — see ITEM LINKING
       NOTES. Generic nouns ("a magic weapon", "a ring", "armor", "a
       potion") are not item names and are not linked.
     - Group mentions that do not name one variety: "Belt of Giant
       Strength", "Ioun Stone", "Instrument of the Bards", "Figurine of
       Wondrous Power", a bare "+1/+2/+3 weapon" range, "Enspelled ..."
       without a tier (see ITEM LINKING NOTES).
     - The plural "Elemental Planes" / "Inner Planes" as a generic group
       (link one specific plane, or lore-inner-planes, instead).
     - Languages (Celestial, Giant, Primordial...) — they are not creature
       types.
     - Individual named monsters now have entries in monsters-data.js
       (monster-{name}); whether a monster name gets linked is decided
       per linking pass. Creature TYPE links (lore-data.js) still only
       cover the 14 broad types, not specific monster species
     - Individual PC races/species (Human, Dwarf, Elf, drow, etc.) —
       lore-data.js only covers the 14 creature TYPES, not species
     - Bare "Extra Attack" or "Spellcasting" when used generically
       across multiple classes without naming one specific class —
       every class has its own differently-numbered feature id for
       these, so a generic cross-class mention has no single correct
       target; only link when a specific class is named alongside it

9. CROSS-DROPDOWN NAVIGATION LINKS ARE LEGITIMATE.
   A mention of another whole Rules-dropdown topic by its functional
   name (not just its exact title) can link to that dropdown's own id
   as a navigational shortcut — e.g. "Mounts" -> dropMountedCombat,
   "learning a language" -> dropLanguages, "the nine Magic Item Tables"
   -> rules-magic-item-tables (the group container, when referring to
   all of them collectively rather than one specific table).


ITEM LINKING NOTES
------------------------
(How to link to items from any file. All ids are listed in the COMPLETE ID
REFERENCE at the bottom — never guess an item id, look it up there.)

WHICH ID FAMILY TO USE
- Mundane gear, weapons, armor, tools, mounts, vehicles, packs, foci, weapon
  properties and weapon masteries  ->  equipment-*   (items-data.js)
- Every magic item  ->  magic-item-*   (magic-items-data.js)
- The 14 poisons  ->  equipment-*   (they are stored in magic-items-data.js but
  keep their equipment- ids: assassin-s-blood, malice, pale-tincture,
  serpent-venom, truth-serum, burnt-othur-fumes, essence-of-ether,
  lolth-s-sting, oil-of-taggit, torpor, carrion-crawler-mucus, midnight-tears,
  purple-worm-poison, wyvern-poison)
- Container ids (items-equipment, items-magic-items, magic-items-{category},
  magic-items-{category}-{rarity}, equipment-{category}) are valid link
  targets for navigation, but a link to a named item always goes to the item.
- The apostrophe rule for item ids is: apostrophe -> hyphen, repeated hyphens
  collapse ("Heward's" = heward-s, "Armourer's" = armourer-s, "Thieves' Tools"
  = thieves-tools, "Oil of Dragon's Bane" = oil-of-dragon-s-bane).

GROUP / VARIANT ITEMS (what a generic mention links to)
- Weapon / Armor / Shield / Ammunition +1, +2, +3: one entry per tier, ids
  magic-item-weapon-1/-2/-3, magic-item-armor-1/-2/-3, magic-item-shield-1/-2/-3,
  magic-item-ammunition-1/-2/-3. "a +3 weapon" -> magic-item-weapon-3. A mention
  that does not name one tier ("a +1, +2, or +3 magic weapon", "a magic weapon")
  has NO target — leave it unlinked.
- Rod of the Pact Keeper, Wand of the War Mage, Wraps of Unarmed Power: id ends
  in -1/-2/-3 (rarity tiers); link only when the bonus is named.
- Enspelled Armor / Staff / Weapon: one entry per rarity tier
  (magic-item-enspelled-{armor|staff|weapon}-{uncommon|rare|very-rare|legendary});
  link only when the tier is named. A generic "Enspelled" mention has no target.
- Ioun Stones: one entry per stone (magic-item-ioun-stone-of-{name}); a generic
  "Ioun Stone" has no target.
- Instrument of the Bards: one entry per instrument
  (magic-item-instrument-of-the-bards-{doss-lute|fochlucan-bandore|
  mac-fuirmidh-cittern|canaith-mandolin|cli-lyre|anstruth-harp|ollamh-harp});
  a generic "Instrument of the Bards" has no target.
- Figurine of Wondrous Power and Quaal's Feather Token: one entry per variety
  (magic-item-figurine-of-wondrous-power-{variety},
  magic-item-quaal-s-feather-token-{variety}); generic mention = no target.
- Belt of Giant Strength: six entries (hill, fire, frost, stone, cloud, storm;
  magic-item-belt-of-{type}-giant-strength). There is NO generic entry, so
  "a Belt of Giant Strength" with no type is not linked.
- Potions of Healing: magic-item-potion-of-healing (plain),
  -potion-of-healing-greater, -superior, -supreme. "Potion of Greater Healing"
  (2014 wording) = magic-item-potion-of-healing-greater.
- Potions of Giant Strength: official entries are
  magic-item-potion-of-giant-strength-hill / -fire / -frost-or-stone / -cloud /
  -storm. magic-item-potion-of-giant-strength-piccolo is the homebrew one; a
  generic "Potion of Giant Strength" is not linked.
- Crystal Ball: magic-item-crystal-ball (plain) and the three variants
  (-of-mind-reading, -of-telepathy, -of-true-seeing).
- Spell Scroll -> magic-item-spell-scroll; Scroll of Protection and Scroll of
  Titan Summoning have their own entries.

SAME-NAME PAIRS (which one a plain mention means)
- "Perfume of Bewitching": magic-item-perfume-of-bewitching-wondrous is the
  official wondrous item and is the target for a plain mention;
  magic-item-perfume-of-bewitching is the homebrew poison.
- "Basic Poison": equipment-poison-basic (the mundane 2024 poison) is the
  target for a plain mention; magic-item-basic-poison is the homebrew poison.
- "Potion of Greater Healing": official = magic-item-potion-of-healing-greater;
  magic-item-potion-of-greater-healing-piccolo is the homebrew twin and is
  never the target of a plain mention.
- "Feather Token": magic-item-feather-token is the Eberron fall-protection
  item; Quaal's Feather Tokens (magic-item-quaal-s-feather-token-*) are a
  different item family. A plain "Feather Token" mention means the Eberron item
  only when it is that item; the generic "Feather Tokens look like feathers"
  text inside the Quaal's entries is not a link.
- "Glamerweave": two entries split by rarity, magic-item-glamerweave-common and
  magic-item-glamerweave-uncommon; link only when the rarity is named.
- Eberron items that also exist as 2014 Artificer infusions with the same name
  (Boots of the Winding Path, Helm of Awareness, Mind Sharpener, Repeating Shot,
  Repulsion Shield, Returning Weapon, Spell-Refueling Ring): the magic-item-*
  entry is the 2024 Forge of the Artificer item and is the target for item
  mentions (e.g. the 2024 Artificer plan tables); the class-2014-artificer-*
  entries are the 2014 infusion rules and are the target only where the 2014
  Infuse Item feature is meant.

ALIASES AND VARIANT WRITINGS
- Plurals: Potions of Healing, Wands of Fireballs, Daggers... link to the
  singular entry.
- 2014 / lowercase text: "bag of holding", "potion of greater healing" are
  matched case-insensitively.
- Weapon and armor names: "Weapon (Longsword)" type lines link the weapon
  (equipment-longsword); armor type lines link equipment-chain-mail-armor for
  "Chain Mail", equipment-scale-mail-armor for "Scale Mail",
  equipment-studded-leather-armor for "Studded Leather",
  equipment-half-plate-armor for "Half Plate", equipment-plate-armor for
  "Plate Armor", and equipment-shield for "Armor (Shield)".
- Poisons: "Drow Poison" (2014 name) = equipment-lolth-s-sting; "Poison, Basic"
  = equipment-poison-basic.
- Weapon properties and masteries inside weapon entries link to
  equipment-property-{ammunition|finesse|heavy|light|loading|range|reach|
  thrown|two-handed|versatile} and equipment-mastery-{cleave|graze|nick|push|
  sap|slow|topple|vex}.

DO NOT LINK AS ITEMS
- Generic nouns used as plain English: staff, ring, rod, wand, armor, shield,
  weapon, ammunition, potion, scroll, oil, rope, book, orb, crystal, light.
  "Quarterstaff" (weapon) is not the Staff magic item family.
- An item's own name inside its own entry (no self-links) and run-in feature
  names written in bold.
- Names that are also ordinary words only when capitalised as the item:
  Wave, Whelm, Defender, Malice, Torpor.

DECISIONS MADE IN THE ITEMS LINKING PASS (so later passes stay consistent)
- Never linked: text inside bold run-in feature names ("Spellcasting.",
  "Giant's Bane."), inside bold labels ("Damage:", "AC:", "HP:", "Properties:"
  as a label), inside the <h5> Legacy heading, and an entry's own name inside
  itself.
- Type lines ("Weapon (Longsword), Rare (requires attunement ...)") link the
  specific weapon/armor and the word "attunement"; a class named after
  "attunement by a" links to that class.
- Class names link to class-2024-{class} in the main text and to
  class-2014-{class} inside a Legacy block. Narrative uses (druids wearing hide
  armor, "legendary bard colleges", a rogue as an NPC card) are not linked.
- Bare "creature", "damage", "object", "target", "spell" and "weapon" are
  not linked; "damage roll", "attack
  roll", "saving throw", "Hit Points", "Bonus Action", "Magic action",
  "Utilize action", "Attack action", "Armor Class"/"AC", "DC", Advantage,
  Disadvantage, Resistance, Immunity, Vulnerability, Initiative, Concentration,
  Darkvision/Truesight/Blindsight/Tremorsense, Bright/Dim Light, Unoccupied
  Space, Difficult Terrain and the 15 conditions are.
- Ordinary-word spell names (Light, Fly, Command, Sleep, Shield, Dawn, Wish,
  Message, Knock, Web, Symbol, Resistance, Magic Weapon, ...) are linked only
  with a casting cue: "cast X", "the X spell", "X (cantrip)", a listed charge
  cost "X (3 charges)", or a cell in a spell table. Multi-word spell names link
  always.
- Creature types link only in their type sense ("against Undead", "a
  Giant"): not inside monster/item names (Giant Rat, Dragon Scale Mail,
  Elemental Plane, Conjure Elemental), not for languages (Celestial, Giant),
  not for ordinary plants/animals.
- First mention per block: Common rules words link once per "block" (the main
  text, the Legacy block, and each table count separately; a long entry with
  three or more bold run-in sections links once per section).
- Ability scores link when used mechanically (Dexterity saving throw, Strength
  score, Constitution modifier -> glossary-constitution). Skills link inside "Ability (Skill)".


DECISIONS MADE IN THE CLASSES LINKING PASS (so later passes stay consistent)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Variants count. Link plurals and inflected forms of every linked term, not
  only the exact singular title: creatures, spells, weapons, cantrips, saving
  throws, attack rolls, ability checks, conditions, proficiencies / proficient,
  Hit Die / Hit Dice / Hit Point Die (-> glossary-hit-point-dice), long rests,
  Opportunity Attacks, Unarmed Strikes, "grapple" (-> glossary-grappling).
- Abbreviations link when used mechanically: DC (glossary-difficulty-class),
  AC (glossary-armor-class), HP (glossary-hit-points), XP
  (glossary-experience-points), CR (glossary-challenge-rating), PB
  (glossary-proficiency), STR/DEX/INT/WIS/CHA and Str/Dex/Int/Wis/Cha in stat
  blocks, CON / Con / Constitution (glossary-constitution).
- Stat-block lines (the <th> rows of monster / companion stat blocks) are
  linked exactly like prose: Armor Class, Hit Points, Challenge Rating,
  Passive Perception, Darkvision, Immunities / Resistances, condition names,
  saving throws, "Skills:" lines. Table labels link on their matching parts
  ("Saving Throw Proficiencies": Saving Throw + Proficiencies); a label that is
  exactly a glossary term links whole ("Armor Training", "Hit Point Die").
- Action names: "Magic action", "Dodge action", etc. link the name AND the word
  "action" separately (name -> its own entry, action -> glossary-action).
  "Attack action" links "Attack" -> glossary-attack-action; "Help action" links
  "Help" -> glossary-help. A generic "attack" is never linked to
  glossary-attack-action. "Short or Long Rest": "Short" -> glossary-short-rest
  and "Long Rest" -> glossary-long-rest.
- Never linked: bare target, object, speed, size, line; "reach" as a verb;
  "help" as a verb; damage-type names; the plain die "d20"; "save" on its own.
- "magic" is linked only when it is the Magic action. "magic item(s)" links to
  items-magic-items.
- Weapon and armor categories: "Simple weapons" / "Martial weapons" with no
  melee/ranged named -> equipment-weapons (link text is "Simple" / "Martial" /
  "Simple and Martial"; the word "weapons" itself -> glossary-weapon).
  "Simple Melee", "Simple Ranged", "Martial Melee", "Martial Ranged" -> the four
  equipment-weapons-* categories. "Light / Medium / Heavy armor" (any
  combination) -> equipment-armor.
- Named features are never split. Link the whole name or nothing: Weapon
  Mastery, Potent Spellcasting, Monster Slayer, Sacred Weapon, Uncanny Dodge,
  Favored Enemy, Cantrip Versatility, Ki-Fueled Attack, Two-Weapon Fighting.
  Fighting Style names link to their feats (feat-two-weapon-fighting,
  feat-great-weapon-fighting, feat-thrown-weapon-fighting, ...). The
  "Ability Score Improvement" feature links to feat-ability-score-improvement,
  not glossary-ability-score. The 2014 optional features in the class tables
  link to their own -optional entries. "Pact of the Blade / Tome / Chain" in
  2014 text -> class-2014-warlock-level-3-pact-boon.
- A class name in "X spell list / X spells" links only when the entry belongs
  to a different class.
- Taglines (the title before " | " that opens a subclass description) are not
  linked.
- Plain-English uses are not linked: nature, history, insight, deception,
  stealth, performance (skills); healing, darkness, adventure, friendly,
  climbing, falling, crawling, curses, unconscious, invisible (as ordinary words).
- rules-glossary-data.js: "Help" / "help" as a verb and a generic "attack" are
  not linked; "magic" next to "item(s)" is not the Magic action.


TECHNICAL PROCESS NOTES (for editing these files safely)
--------------------------------------------------------
- Before starting any linking pass: build a full id+title reference map
  by extracting every entry from every *-data.js file with a small
  Python script (parse the "const X_DATA = {...}" JS wrapper as JSON,
  walk entries/children recursively, and for classes-data.js also
  regex-scan each entry's own html for nested
  <details id="..."><summary>...</summary> blocks, since subclass
  content is literal HTML, not JSON children). This turns "does this
  ID exist, and what edition" from a guess into an instant lookup.
  See scripts/extract_ids.py in the dm-screen-linking skill for a
  ready-to-run version of this.
- rules-data.js is a JS file containing one big JSON object. When
  editing it as raw text (not by re-serializing the whole JSON), the
  file's own literal newlines inside string values appear as the
  two-character sequence backslash-n (\n), and literal double quotes
  inside string values appear as the two-character sequence
  backslash-quote (\"). An exact-match find/replace must match these
  literal escape sequences, not real newline/quote characters, or the
  match will silently fail with "0 occurrences found". index.html, by
  contrast, is plain HTML — real newlines and real quotes — no special
  escaping needed there.
- Use a small exact-match-with-uniqueness-check Python helper (find the
  old string, assert it occurs exactly once, replace it, write the
  file) rather than a long chain of manual edits. This catches a wrong
  or already-applied edit immediately instead of silently corrupting
  or skipping content.
- Write edits in small, focused Python scripts (a handful of edits at
  a time) rather than one giant script — if the whole script fails to
  parse (e.g. an apostrophe breaking Python's own string quoting), NONE
  of the edits in that script run, even ones that looked unrelated to
  the syntax error. After any script failure, don't just fix the one
  broken line and move on — re-check whether any of the OTHER edits in
  that same failed script also failed to apply, and recover them too.
- After each batch: reparse the file as JSON (for rules-data.js) or
  check tag balance (for index.html) to confirm nothing broke.
- Before delivering: confirm every data-ref in the edited file(s)
  resolves to a real id somewhere on the site (cross-check against the
  full id reference), confirm there are no duplicate ids introduced,
  and confirm entry/child counts match the pre-edit structure.


REGENERATING THE ID REFERENCE
--------------------------------
If the COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below is suspected stale (a data file has
been edited since), regenerate it with scripts/extract_ids.py from the
dm-screen-linking skill against freshly uploaded copies of the nine
*-data.js files, then replace the relevant section(s) below.


BEFORE ADDING A LINK, ALWAYS CHECK
--------------------------------------
Predicting an ID from the patterns above (or finding it in the
COMPLETE ID REFERENCE) is a starting point, not a guarantee. Before
finalizing a link:
1. Is the word being used in a rules-mechanical sense here, or just
   ordinary narrative/descriptive language? Only link the former.
   ("Elemental magic" should not link to the elemental creature type;
   "magic" should only link to the Magic action when it specifically
   means that action.)
2. Does the exact wording/edition of the target actually match what's
   being described? (See the classes-data.js edition-choice note above.)
3. Is this an ultra-common word that's already been linked once nearby
   in this same entry/section? If so, consider whether a repeat link
   adds real value or just visual noise (see heuristic #2 above).
4. If there's any real uncertainty about whether an entry — or a
   specific edition's version of it — exists at all, and it isn't in
   the COMPLETE ID REFERENCE below, upload/search the actual file
   rather than linking a guessed ID.


COMPLETE ID REFERENCE
=============================================================================
Every ID on the site as of this pass, grouped by source file. Format is
"id<TAB>Title" (Title is the entry's own display title, not necessarily
the exact text used at every link site — see "visible text doesn't have
to match" above). Includes group/category/letter container ids as well
as individual entries — containers are real, clickable <details> targets
too (e.g. lore-outer-planes is used as a link target elsewhere on the
site), even though most links will point at individual entries.
8,753 ids total, zero duplicates across the whole site.
